Roadmap

For completed items, see the Release History below.

The items below describe development directions and priorities. Not all are planned for immediate delivery. Some are long-term efforts spanning multiple major releases, and items may be reprioritized based on engineering capacity, enterprise customer requirements, and community feedback.

Software-Defined Networking

Goal: Bring the remaining tech-preview parts of the SDN stack to full production readiness, with deep platform integration and end-to-end traffic management.

  • Stabilize DHCP-based IP address management (tech preview since Proxmox VE 8.1) and broaden coverage beyond Simple zones.
  • Stabilize and extend external IPAM/DNS plugin integrations (NetBox, phpIPAM, PowerDNS).
  • Add explicit VRF (Virtual Routing and Forwarding) support as a first-class SDN primitive.
  • Add north-south L7 ingress for HTTP/TLS traffic into guests:
    • Hostname- and path-based routing to backend VMs and containers.
    • Built-in ACME-managed certificate handling, extending the existing node-level ACME integration to cover ingress hostnames.
  • Deeper integration between Proxmox SDN and Proxmox Firewall:
    • Graduate the nftables-based firewall (tech preview since Proxmox VE 8.2) toward production readiness, eventually replacing the legacy iptables-based firewall.
    • Expand firewall support for forwarded traffic filtering on host and VNet levels.
    • Integrate auto-generated SDN IP sets into firewall rule management.
  • SDN user experience improvements:
    • Polished overview of zones, VNets, fabrics, controllers, and IPAM entries with their interrelations, including search and filtering.
    • Status graphs and topology visualizations (traffic, BGP/EVPN/OSPF session state, learned MACs and IPs, route propagation).
    • Streamlined configuration wizards for common topologies (single-site EVPN, full-mesh fabric, multi-site exit nodes).

Cluster Resource Scheduler (CRS) & HA

Goal: Round out workload placement, balancing, and HA controls into a unified resource management framework covering all guest types.

  • Extend the dynamic load balancer released in Proxmox VE 9.2 to also actively rebalance non-HA-managed guests.
  • Improve affinity rules:
    • Richer expressivity (soft vs. hard, group-level, topology-aware).
    • Apply affinity rules beyond HA-managed services as part of a unified resource manager.
  • Expose richer per-rule and per-resource tuning of the dynamic scheduler in the web interface.
  • Improve scheduler observability: surface why a particular placement or rebalancing decision was made.

Project "Cattle and Pets"

Goal: Make Proxmox VE excellent for managing large-scale deployments with hundreds or thousands of guests.

Cluster-wide operations
  • Add a cluster-wide operations and status control center as a single pane for orchestrating the actions described below.
  • Assist on Ceph upgrades with semi-automated, ordered restarts of monitors, managers, MDS, and OSDs.
  • Improve user and virtual machine management experience in large deployments (search, filtering, pagination, large-cluster GUI performance).
Bulk operations on guests
  • Bulk configuration editing of attributes (memory, CPU, network bridge, balloon, MTU, firewall flag, tags) across selected guests in one operation.
  • Bulk firewall and security-group application across selected guests.
  • Bulk cloud-init customization (DNS, search domain, SSH-key rotation, upgrade flag) across batches of cloud-init-enabled VMs.
  • Smart-view bulk actions: save a filter as a named view, then apply bulk actions to its members dynamically.
Scheduling and automation
  • Provide a generic scheduling framework, building on the existing calendar-event scheduler used by backup, replication, and verification jobs, as the foundation for periodic operations.
  • Periodic snapshot policies with retention, modeled on backup jobs (hourly/daily/weekly keep-rules).
  • Power schedules for guests and pools (time-based start/stop for dev environments, training labs, business-hours workloads).
  • Scheduled and queued migrations with cancel/preview before execution.
  • Rolling cluster maintenance orchestration (drain → reboot → repopulate → next node, HA-aware) on top of the scheduling framework.
Discovery and observability at scale
  • Auto-tagging rules that apply tags based on guest properties (OS type, network membership, owner, machine type, storage backing).
  • Top-N consumer views for CPU, memory, disk-IO, and network, cluster-wide and per-pool.
  • Cluster-wide event and activity feed with unified, chronological view across all nodes.
  • Per-cluster audit log with actor attribution, retained beyond task-log rotation and exportable for compliance use.
Outbound automation
  • Webhook events for guest lifecycle (created, destroyed, state change, config change, migrated) for CMDB and orchestrator integration.
  • Centralized hookscript management in the web interface, with a node-wide registry of available scripts.
  • Richer server-side API filtering, sorting and pagination for guest-listing endpoints.
Lifecycle and safety
  • Guest deletion protection and soft-delete with grace period, analogous to protected backups.
  • Configuration drift detection against source profile or template, with optional reconciliation.
Provisioning workflows
  • Mass-deployment wizard: deploy N copies of a template with sequential name patterns and IP allocations, with optional per-instance cloud-init customization.
  • Per-pool and per-tag quotas enforcing CPU, memory, and storage allocation limits.
Resource Pools enhancements
  • Improve visual representation of nested pool hierarchy on the web UI.
  • Provide bulk actions for all virtual guests in a resource pool.
  • Support simple resource limits (CPU, memory, storage) for virtual guests in a resource pool.

Health Checks

Goal: Provide proactive, system-wide visibility into the health of all managed resources.

  • General health-check framework covering hosts (pending updates, free storage, RAID/disk state, network and time sync, certificate validity), virtual guests, storages, and cluster-level services (Ceph, corosync, SDN).
  • Guest configuration validation and linting: surface problematic configurations (unused disks, mismatched MTU, deprecated machine types, missing virtio drivers signaled by OS type, oversubscription) as actionable warnings.
  • Surface check results in the web interface with actionable hints and pointers to documentation.
  • Integrate health-check results into the notification system.

Storage & Snapshots

Goal: Make modern snapshot semantics first-class, broaden replication coverage, and improve shared-block-storage ergonomics.

  • Bring "snapshots as volume chains" (tech preview since Proxmox VE 9.0) out of tech preview on LVM-thick, Directory, NFS, and CIFS storages, including support for online removal of the top-most snapshot.
  • Lift the remaining restriction that prevents migrating VMs with snapshots on local volume-chain-enabled storages.
  • Extend the storage replication framework beyond ZFS, building on the snapshot semantics now available via volume chains.
  • Improve multipath integration and setup experience for Fibre Channel and iSCSI deployments.

Virtualization & Guest Management

Goal: Expand virtualization capabilities and streamline guest lifecycle management.

  • Mature OCI image support and provide declarative multi-guest application support based on the Compose Specification, allowing groups of LXC application containers (and, longer-term, VMs) to be defined, deployed, and lifecycle-managed as a single application unit directly from Proxmox VE.
  • Add default configuration profiles for new VMs and containers, allowing administrators to pre-define and reuse sensible defaults (CPU type, machine version, network, storage layout) instead of repeating them on each guest creation.
  • Improve the remote viewer for VM, container, and host consoles, with native RDP-based access as an additional option alongside noVNC and SPICE for better performance and Windows-guest experience.
  • Lift current restrictions on virtiofs-enabled VMs (live migration, snapshots with RAM, hibernation) as upstream QEMU support matures.
  • Expand confidential computing support (AMD SEV-SNP, Intel TDX) to reduce current limitations around live migration, attestation flows, and unsupported feature combinations.
  • Continue improving PCI/GPU passthrough, including broader vGPU live-migration support beyond NVIDIA.
  • Continue improving cross-hypervisor migration workflows (ESXi/vSphere import, OVF/OVA vendor-extension coverage).

Installation

Goal: Cover the remaining enterprise deployment scenarios out of the box.

  • Add native full-disk encryption (LUKS) for root filesystems in the installer, for both legacy-BIOS and UEFI/Secure Boot setups.
  • Continue extending automated/unattended installation: richer post-install hooks, PXE/iPXE integration, and authenticated answer-file delivery.

Long-term strategic directions

Multi-year engineering efforts that underpin many of the items above.

  • Continue the gradual migration of Perl backend components to Rust, building on the perlmod interop layer and shared Rust libraries already used across Proxmox products.
  • Continue the long-term migration of the main web interface from ExtJS to the Rust/Yew-based Proxmox widget toolkit, which already powers the Proxmox VE mobile UI, Proxmox Datacenter Manager, and the Proxmox Mail Gateway quarantine UI.

Release History

See also Announcement forum

Proxmox VE 9.2 for 64-bit ARM (arm64)

Released 05. August 2026: See Downloads

  • Based on Debian Trixie (13.6)
  • Latest 7.0 Kernel as stable default
  • QEMU 11.0
  • LXC 7.0
  • ZFS 2.4
  • Ceph Tentacle 20.2.3

Highlights

  • First Proxmox VE release for a CPU architecture besides x86-64 (amd64).
  • Full and official support for enterprise arm64 systems, currently the NVIDIA Grace and NVIDIA Vera platforms. Other UEFI-based ARMv9-A or newer hardware is supported on a best-effort basis.
  • Shares the code base, package repositories, and release lifecycle with the x86-64 builds. Configuration, tooling, and documentation are identical, except for a small set of architecture-specific differences.
  • Hosts must boot through UEFI and describe their hardware through ACPI. Device-tree-only single-board computers, such as the Raspberry Pi, are not supported.
  • Some x86-specific features are unavailable, among them SeaBIOS for VMs, AMD SEV, and Intel GVT-g. Live migration is only possible between nodes of the same architecture.

This is a one-time entry for the initial arm64 release. Subsequent releases cover both architectures and are not listed separately anymore.

Proxmox VE 9.2

Released 21. May 2026: See Downloads

  • Based on Debian Trixie (13.5)
  • Latest 7.0 Kernel as new stable default
  • QEMU 11.0
  • LXC 7.0
  • ZFS 2.4
  • Ceph Squid 19.2.3
  • Ceph Tentacle 20.2.1

Highlights

  • Dynamic Load Balancing with the Cluster Resource Scheduler (CRS).
    In dynamic scheduling mode, the CRS uses real-time node and guest resource utilization metrics to balance HA-managed guests.
    The new load balancer can automatically migrate HA-managed guests to lower the overall node imbalance across the cluster.
    The behavior and sensitivity of the load balancer can be tuned with various parameters in the HA panel or the datacenter options.
  • Numerous improvements to the Software Defined Networking (SDN) stack:
    Add WireGuard and BGP as new fabric protocols.
    Add support for route maps and prefix lists for fine-grained BGP/EVPN route filtering.
    Further additions include route redistribution for OSPF fabrics, additional options for configuring EVPN controllers, and IPv6 underlay support for EVPN.
  • Management of custom CPU models from the web interface.
    A new section in the Datacenter → Guest Resources/Hardware allows creating, editing, and removing custom CPU models from the web interface.
    Custom CPU models allow tailoring the virtual CPU exposed to VMs to a specific set of features.
    The VM CPU flags selector indicates the supported flags on each cluster node, making cluster-wide compatibility issues easier to spot up front.
  • Disarm and arm HA for safer cluster maintenance.
    The HA stack can now be disarmed cluster-wide via the new disarm-ha and arm-ha CRM commands.
    This allows for planned, cluster-wide maintenance work, such as changes in the cluster network, without triggering any node fencing events.
    The HA resource state is preserved during the disarm window so HA resources return to their previous state after the maintenance is completed.
  • Kernel 7.0 as new stable default.
  • Ceph Tentacle 20.2.1 is available as stable option.

Changelog Overview

Before upgrading, please consider Known Issues & Breaking Changes.

Enhancements in the web interface (GUI)

  • Expose the parallel worker count for all bulk actions, not only for bulk migrations.
    An empty value falls back to the new auto default, which preserves backwards-compatibility with older nodes in a mixed-version cluster.
  • Make several improvements to the task viewer:
    • Add a Download button to download a task log directly without opening the full task viewer popup (issue 7087).
    • Derive the task end-time from the polled task status if the caller did not provide it, ensuring finished tasks show the correct duration.
  • Make various improvements to the login flow:
    • Preserve deep-link fragments (e.g. /#vNNN) across an OpenID Connect login to make users land on the requested resource (issue 7337).
    • Prevent the realm selection from being changed on the login page as a side-effect of unrelated user creation (issue 7149).
    • Reset the UI state on automatic logout to protect users who share a workstation.
  • Allow creating snapshots and backups directly from the resource tree context menu (issue 6209).
  • Improve the rendering of nested pools in the resource view by grouping the child pools under their parent pool.
  • Add an architecture column to the resource store, enabling other views to display the architecture of a node.
  • Fix the OSD toolbar overflowing on narrow viewports.
  • Fix the CephFS page not being scrollable when multiple MDS are configured (issue 6234).
  • Fix sorting of the Two-Factor Authentication (TFA) column in the datacenter, permission, and user grids (issue 7191).
  • Fix the network section being unavailable in the folder view (issue 7125).
  • Improve the "Confirm Remove" dialog layout to properly accommodate larger fields and components.
  • Allow creating network bonds without specifying the bond-primary option to match the backend, which treats the value as optional (issue 4911).
  • Use the correct IANA timezone name Europe/Kyiv in the timezone selector (issue 7141).
  • Smaller fixes and improvements:
    • Fix parsing of property strings whose values contain an equals sign.
    • Trim trailing whitespace from custom filenames when downloading ISOs, so the file extension is recognized correctly.
    • Include Linux Kernel 7.x in the version range for the VM OS selector.
    • Fix a typo in a translated OVMF-related error message.
  • Improvements to the mobile web interface:
    • Add a basic firewall view to the cluster, node, virtual machine, and container pages, including an editable rules panel.
    • Container pages now use the shared network and options panels, and align their layout with the virtual machine pages.
    • Add the advanced audiences field to the OpenID Connect realm editor (issue 5076).
    • Fix the OpenID Connect redirect handler to URL-decode the received code value (issue 7290).
    • Fix the CPU utilization displayed on the dashboard (issue 7330).
    • Show the subscription level consistently under nodes and on the resources page.

Virtual machines (KVM/QEMU)

  • New QEMU version 11.0:
    See the upstream changelog for details.
  • Management of custom CPU models from the web interface.
    A new section in the Datacenter → Guest Resources/Hardware allows creating, editing, and removing custom CPU models from the web interface.
    Custom CPU models allow tailoring the virtual CPU exposed to VMs to a specific set of features.
    The VM CPU flags selector indicates the supported flags on each cluster node, making cluster-wide compatibility issues easier to spot up front.
  • Improve the user experience for the Microsoft and Windows 2023 certificate enrollment procedure.
    In addition to the CLI command qm enroll-efi-keys, the enrollment procedure can now also be done via the web interface and API.
    The Windows UEFI CA 2023 and Microsoft KEK CA 2023 certificates are also included in the enrollment procedure now.
    Upon starting a VM, users are now always warned about missing Microsoft certificate enrollments for EFI disks with pre-enrolled keys.
  • Allow taking and removing live snapshots of VMs with TPM state on storages with snapshots as volume chains (issue 7066).
    However, because of current technical limitations, the top-most snapshot cannot be removed while the VM is running.
  • Add the QEMU guest agent option freeze-fs to control whether the freeze and thaw commands are issued on the guest filesystem during backups, clones, replications, and snapshots (issue 1964).
    The previous freeze-fs-on-backup and guest-fsfreeze options are kept as aliases for the new option for backwards compatibility.
    The task log for these actions additionally informs users when the freeze and thaw commands are issued now.
  • Improve the QEMU guest agent file reading functionality by allowing larger file reads while avoiding inflated payload sizes for binary data.
    The count and offset options specify the amount of bytes that are read and at which byte offset the read operation starts.
    The decode option controls whether the returned bytes are decoded to raw bytes or are kept in their base64 encoding. Keeping the returned bytes in the base64 encoding can significantly reduce the payload size for binary data.
  • Improve the runtime of the VM memory usage lookup for VMs with large amounts of memory (issue 6935).
    As kernel same-page merging (KSM) significantly increases the lookup time, the mem and memhost status properties do not take account of it.
  • Support VMs with the TPM state stored on iSCSI storages and ZFS over iSCSI storages (issue 3662).
  • Extend and improve the experimental VM support for the aarch64 architecture.
    This exposes the CPU model, flags, and machine types for ARM CPUs and ensures that relevant code paths fallback to the correct architecture.
    Furthermore, this disables certain architecture-specific features, such as S3/S4 ACPI power states and HyperV enlightenments, for ARM-based VMs.
  • Allow the live migration of VMs with enabled VNC clipboards and QEMU machine version 10.1.
  • Allow longer-running snapshots of large qcow2 files on file-based network storages by increasing the timeout for snapshot-related QMP commands.
  • Restrict the migration option downtime-limit to a maximum of 2000 seconds (issue 7044).
  • Fix an issue, which caused conntrack-related error messages if HA-managed VMs are migrated to other nodes due to the shutdown policy migrate (issue 7092).
  • Fix a race condition, which happened during parallel migrations with conntrack state tracking.
  • Fix an issue, which caused VMs fail to migrate if a previous migration with conntrack state tracking has not been cleaned up properly.
  • Fix an issue, which caused live-restored VMs to shutdown if they are added as HA resources at the same time.
  • Fix an issue, which caused HA-managed VMs to ignore the requested status during a restore operation.
  • Prevent VM cleanups during an ongoing snapshot rollback operation to avoid inconsistent snapshot states.
  • Prevent cloud-init disks to be renamed during migrations to avoid the disk no longer being recognized as a cloud-init disk afterwards.
  • Fix an issue, which caused live migrations using migration type insecure to fail for IPv6 target hosts.
  • Fix an issue, which caused spurious atomic file errors during VM migrations.
  • Fix an issue, where post-hook scripts were not executed when a VM is shutdown within the guest operating system itself (issue 7119).
  • Fix a boot issue for Windows guests with Virtualization-based Security (VBS) enabled by disabling the cet-ibt and cet-ss CPU flags.
  • Switch from the deprecated block-job-complete to the newer job-complete QMP command.
  • Require users to have the VM.Config.Cloudinit privilege to dump the cloud-init password (PSA-2026-00017-1).
  • Require users to have the VM.PowerMgmt privilege to start VMs after VM creation, restoration, and snapshot rollbacks.
    This is only relevant for custom roles as the PVE-shipped roles do not split VM.Allocate, VM.Backup, or VM.Snapshot.Rollback from VM.PowerMgmt.
  • Escape commas in the URL-encoded fields for serial devices and SMBIOS fields.
  • Add additional checks for migration tunnel socket paths to harden against malicious actors.
  • Remove redundant checks for USB hotplug support and unplug events during unrelated VM configuration changes.
  • Warn users about OVMF VMs with large memory address spaces, but use qemu64- and kvm64-derived CPU models without the pdpe1gb CPU flag.
  • Warn users about the missing rdseed feature for some Zen 5 host CPUs with outdated firmware on Linux kernel versions 6.17.9 and newer.
  • Make the disk controller default to IDE when adding a second CD-ROM drive for Windows VirtIO drivers in the VM create wizard (issue 7599).
  • Use the nvml-wrapper and nvml-wrapper-sys packages to query vGPU-related information.
  • Improve the user-facing documentation of various VM-related options.
  • Improve the information provided by task logs for various VM operations.
    • The new VMID for linked clones is now included in the task log for VM clones.
    • The target disk name is now included in the task log for VM disk reassignments.
    • Add additional log messages about important operations in the task log for VM disk movements.
    • Add additional log message about the target volume identifier in the task log for VM disk clones.
  • Add an additional syslog message about a manual qm unlock invocation with the relevant VMID.
    • Fix a spurious error message during the migration OVMF VMs.
    • Improve the user-facing error message upon starting a VM backup.
    • Improve the user-facing error messages caused by failed block jobs.
    • Improve the user-facing error message caused by hot-unplugging devices, which are likely still used in the guest.
    • Do not log a confusing warning about a missing host_mtu setting in scenarios, where this is expected.

Containers (LXC)

  • New LXC version 7.0:
    See the upstream changelog for details.
  • Add the mount point option idmap to configure the UID/GID mappings on individual mount points without affecting the global container mapping.
    Per-mountpoint idmappings only work for unprivileged containers, while privileged containers remain unaffected.
  • Add the mount point option keepattrs to control whether the mounted filesystem inherits the ownership and permissions from the existing mount point directory.
    This might be helpful for some OCI container images, where some directories are expected to have certain user-controlled permissions.
  • Deprecate and warn about raw cgroup v1 entries in the container configuration.
    The support for cgroup v1 will be removed in a future release.
  • Honor the OCI image User property, which allows control over which user and group the process runs as by default.
  • Allow CentOS-based containers to configure multiple name servers (issue 7156).
  • Prevent local "copy.fail" privilege escalation exploit via AF_ALG sockets within containers by using seccomp filtering (PSA-2026-00018-1).
    If AF_ALG support is required in a specific container, see the security advisory forum post for more details.
  • Fix an issue, where containers failed to start if the architecture field was set to an empty string.
  • Setup systemd-networkd for newer SUSE-based containers like OpenSUSE Leap 16.0 (issue 7487).
  • Fix an issue, which caused IPAM entries to be duplicated for the old VMID instead of the new VMID for a cloned container (issue 7568).
  • Switch from the deprecated /etc/timezone to the recommended timedatectl to initialize the containers timezone setting (issue 7175).
  • Mount a tmpfs at /dev/shm for application containers to align with the OCI Runtime specification defaults.
  • Improve the user-facing documentation of various container-related options.
  • Improve the information provided by task logs for various container operations.
    • The new VMID for linked clones is now included in the task log for container clones.
    • Add an additional syslog message about a manual pct unlock invocation with the relevant VMID.
    • Add a warning that nesting should be enabled for containers, which require systemd nesting to isolate services (issue 6897).

General improvements for virtual guests

  • Disallow the remote migration of HA resources to other external nodes, regardless of their current state.
    If HA-managed VMs or containers should be migrated to remote nodes, these should be removed from the HA stack before the remote migration.
  • Fix security issues concerning the VNC-related API endpoints that would allow sufficiently privileged attackers to hijack VNC sessions and guess the effective VNC password in certain scenarios (PSA-2026-00014-1).
    External clients connecting directly to a VNC port opened via the vncshell or vncproxy API endpoints have to adapt to some breaking changes, which are detailed in the security advisory forum post.
  • Require users to have the Sys.Console privilege to add VMs or containers as HA resources during their creation or restoration (PSA-2026-00015-1).

HA Manager

  • Dynamic Load Balancing with the Cluster Resource Scheduler (CRS) (issue 6243).
    In dynamic scheduling mode, the CRS uses real-time node and guest resource utilization metrics to balance HA-managed guests.
    The new load balancer can automatically migrate HA-managed guests to lower the overall node imbalance across the cluster.
    The behavior and sensitivity of the load balancer can be tuned with various parameters in the HA panel or the datacenter options.
  • Disarm and arm HA for safer cluster maintenance (issue 2751).
    The HA stack can now be disarmed cluster-wide via the new disarm-ha and arm-ha CRM commands.
    This allows for planned, cluster-wide maintenance work, such as changes in the cluster network, without triggering any node fencing events.
    The HA resource state is preserved during the disarm window so HA resources return to their previous state after the maintenance is completed.
  • Ensure that manual HA resource migrations do not violate the HA node affinity rules (issue 1497).
    This prevents manual HA resource migrations, which would result in immediate migrations to allowed nodes.
  • Include the utilization metrics of all guests in the load accounting for all CRS scheduling modes.
  • Ensure that the type prefix of HA resources corresponds to the type of the guest.
  • Ensure that the nodenames provided by any CRM commands exist before these are committed.
  • Fix an issue, where removed HA resources are stuck in the deleting state if the HA Manager is idle.
  • Ensure that HA rules always have their required properties set to a valid value (issue 7399).
  • Improve the runtime of the migration of HA groups to HA node affinity rules for larger cluster setups (issue 7133).
  • Add additional helpful information to the watchdog-mux service's log output.
  • Fix a bug, which caused HA resources in a positive resource affinity rule to migrate back to their previous node after these have already been moved to the target node if another HA resource part of the rule is still being migrated (issue 6801).
  • Fix an issue, where a malformed, empty crs datacenter option would result in a CRM error.
  • Improve the user-facing documentation of various HA-related options.

Backup/Restore

  • Deprecate the legacy starttime and dow parameters of the backup job API in favor of schedule.
    The legacy parameters were superseded by schedule since Proxmox VE 7.1 and have now been removed from the API response.
  • Improve the backup-job guest selection web interface (issue 4490).
    A new search field filters the guest grid by name or VMID, a review toggle hides unselected guests, and a counter below the grid shows the current selection size.
    When the job is in exclude-selected mode, the label above the guest grid now reflects this so it is clear at a glance.
    The same shared search widget is also used in the disk-tree details and backed-guests views for a consistent experience.

Storage

  • Show sizes for qcow2 volumes on shared LVM storages without having to activate each logical volume (issue 6819).
    As activating each LV is costly per-volume and may not be possible on shared LVM, where another node may already have the volume active, the API, CLI, and storage content view now report an approximate size for inactive qcow2 volumes instead.
  • Surface clearer errors when creating a content subdirectory on a storage fails.
    Failures now name the content type whose directory could not be created and include the underlying error, permission-denied and read-only-filesystem failures hint that the storage needs read and write access.
  • Add a /nodes/{node}/storage/{storage}/identity API endpoint that returns the instance ID of a Proxmox Backup Server storage.
    Intended for tools such as the Proxmox Datacenter Manager to map PBS storages onto their corresponding PBS remotes.
  • Validate the ZFS pool blocksize parameter against a new schema format (issue 3936).
    Accepts integers between 512 B and 16 MiB with optional k/m suffixes and enforces that the value is a power of two, surfacing misconfigurations before pool creation.
  • Fix the CIFS storage connection check failing for Kerberos-authenticated shares.
    With smbclient 4.22 (shipped in Debian Trixie), the previous guest-user fallback no longer succeeds. Kerberos is now detected from the sec=krb5 mount option, and the connection check uses native Kerberos authentication via --use-kerberos.
  • Fix the PBS storage plugin not recognizing valid Proxmox Backup Server API tokens with names or realms starting with a digit or underscore, or with single-character names (issue 6900).
  • Fix the LVM plugin hanging on the storage lock when performing a snapshot rollback through the CLI on storages with saferemove and snapshot-as-volume-chain.
  • Fix a regression in the import/export format handling for images content, which broke offline migration of guest volumes.
  • Run the OVF/OVA XML parser inside a forked process restricted by a seccomp filter as a defense-in-depth measure for guest imports.
  • Fix the OCI registry pull dialog potentially hanging due to catastrophic regular expression backtracking when parsing image references.

Ceph

  • Add support for Ceph Tentacle 20.2.
  • Switch the default Ceph release for fresh installations to 20.2 Tentacle.
    pveceph install on a Ceph-free node and the wizard's pre-selected release on a Ceph-free cluster now both default to Tentacle.
    Existing clusters are unaffected, as the wizard's cluster-version probe continues to pin new nodes to whichever release the cluster already runs.
    Users can upgrade from Ceph Squid to Tentacle by following the upgrade guide.
  • When installing an experimental Ceph release, pveceph now warns if the default enterprise repository is selected and prompts the user to switch to the test repository.
  • Fix Ceph pool edit dialog displaying incorrect replication size values (issue 7266).
    Opening the pool edit dialog could show incorrect Size and Min Size values, potentially leading to accidental data loss if the configuration was saved without noticing the discrepancy.
  • Fix Ceph monitor log ownership not being set correctly when adding a new monitor (issue 7011).
    Effective monitor logging on freshly set up clusters previously required a subsequent ceph-common package upgrade.
  • Fix OSD creation failing when auth_client_required is absent from ceph.conf.
  • Fix pveceph purge deleting Ceph configuration and keyring files on cluster nodes that are not running a monitor.
    A faulty membership check always reported the local node as a monitor, causing the foreign-monitor guard to never trigger.
  • Fix Ceph pool statistics showing data from the wrong pool.
    A broken filter on the ceph df response caused the API to return the statistics of whichever pool appeared last in the output, regardless of the requested pool.


Access control

  • Support regenerating the secret of an existing API token in-place.
    This allows rotating a leaked or compromised token without having to delete and re-create it, preserving all associated ACL entries.
  • Fix LDAP-synced user attributes containing non-ASCII characters being double-encoded and displaying garbled in the GUI and CLI.
  • Fix group name validation allowing invalid characters that could corrupt the user configuration file (issue 7102).
  • Support an optional list of additional accepted audiences for OpenID realms (issue 5076).
    The new audiences parameter improves compatibility with providers that include extra audience claims in their ID tokens.

Firewall & Software Defined Networking

  • Add WireGuard as a new SDN fabric protocol.
    WireGuard fabrics provide encrypted node-to-node tunnels usable as a secure underlay for VXLAN networks, migration networks, and cross-cluster connectivity.
    Cryptographic keys are generated and managed automatically per node.
    External (non-Proxmox VE) peers can be added to the fabric for interoperability with other WireGuard endpoints.
  • Add BGP as a new SDN fabric protocol.
    BGP fabrics use an eBGP unnumbered underlay where each node is assigned a unique ASN and peers over physical interfaces without requiring IP addresses on fabric links.
  • Add support for route maps and prefix lists in the SDN configuration.
    BGP and EVPN controllers can now be configured with custom route maps for selective route export and filtering.
    Route maps and prefix lists are managed as first-class SDN objects with their own API endpoints.
  • Add route redistribution for OSPF SDN fabrics.
    OSPF fabrics can now be configured to redistribute connected, local, kernel, and BGP-learned routes into OSPF, facilitating multi-protocol routing architectures.
  • Extend EVPN controller functionality.
    Multiple EVPN controllers can now be configured per cluster, enabling Inter-AS Option B/C configurations per RFC 4364.
    Controller deployment can now be restricted to specific nodes.
    BGP session types and eBGP multihop settings are now configurable per controller.
  • Add IPv6 underlay support for EVPN controllers.
    EVPN controllers can now be configured with IPv6 peer addresses. As with VXLAN, IPv6 is preferred when the underlying fabric provides both address families; mixing IPv4 and IPv6 peer addresses within the same zone is not supported.
  • Allow dry-running SDN configuration changes before applying them.
  • Fix ipset lookup for auto-generated IPAM ipsets.
  • Fix firewall rule comments that contain hash characters being truncated or misread (issue 6399).
  • Fix a dnsmasq crash when a subnet has no gateway configured (issue 5949).
  • Fix IPv6 exit-node local routing in EVPN networks.
  • Fix VXLAN zone local-node detection when an IPv6 underlay is used.
    The local underlay selection previously only matched the IPv4 address key, so a peer list consisting of IPv6 addresses never resolved to the local node.
  • Fix BGP IPv6 network address having a spurious double slash in the generated FRR configuration.
  • Fix SDN configuration not being regenerated when physical NIC names change for VLAN/QinQ zones on non-VLAN-aware bridges.
    This is particularly relevant in combination with network interface name pinning.
  • Fix SDN zones and fabrics not appearing in the ACL permission path selector in the UI (issue 7300).
  • Fix VLAN/QinQ bridge port detection (issue 6806).

Improved management of Proxmox VE nodes

  • Allow attaching a physical location to make it easier to identify and navigate to physical sites in distributed setups.
    The physical location can be configured for the whole cluster or for each node individually.
    The rendered location includes a link to the corresponding place on OpenStreetMap.

Installation ISO

  • Add support for preparing PXE- and iPXE-compatible auto installation ISOs.
    This can be done with the new --pxe and --pxe-loader options for the proxmox-auto-install-assistant prepare-iso command.
    This functionality requires at least 6 GiB of available memory on the host.
  • Add the auto installer option --answer-auth-token to embed an HTTP authorization token into the prepared installation ISO.
    The token is sent in the HTTP 'Authorization' header when retrieving the answer file.
    This allows an answer server such as the Proxmox Datacenter Manager to authenticate which ISO is asking for which answer file.
  • Correctly handle IPv6-only setups by selecting the management interface, address, gateway, and name servers based on the default route's IP family.
    Additionally, send IPv6 router solicitations via rdisc6 and run rdnssd at startup to allow picking up SLAAC-assigned addresses and router-advertised DNS resolvers on networks without DHCPv4.
  • Add the auto-install-assistant subcommand inspect-iso, which allows users to verify an existing installation ISO.
  • Add the answer file property subscription-key to allow staging a subscription key on the system for activation on first boot.
  • Add the optional post-hook answer file property auth-token to allow sending an HTTP authentication token to the post-hook target.
  • Prefer the JSON format over TOML format when fetching answer files over HTTP connections.
  • Accept IPv6 prefix lengths of up to /128, which was previously capped to only /126
  • Make the network interface name validation consistent with the API schema.
  • Fix a bug which caused the auto installer mark uppercase MAC addresses as unknown for network interface pinning maps.
  • Fix a 'command not found' error that could occur under certain circumstances.
  • Save the post-hook data to /run/proxmox-installer/post-hook-data.json in the installer environment.
  • Allow users to drop to the debug shell by pressing Ctrl-C during the installation.
  • Make the post-hook procedure more failure tolerant by defaulting to safe values if some partial information could not be gathered.
  • Improve the error messages if fetching the answer file failed by including the full server response body.

Notable changes and general improvements

  • New corosync clusters are created with a lower token_coefficient of 125ms to reduce the time needed for reestablishing a new cluster membership after a node failure.
  • The cluster certificate helpers now verify that a newly set certificate and its key actually match by loading them into a TLS context before applying them.
    This avoids confusing failures where pveproxy would start up but fail to serve HTTPS, manifesting as "broken pipe" errors on other cluster nodes.
  • The pvereport tool now uses zarcstat instead of the deprecated arcstat, matching the ZFS 2.4 rename.
  • Unify the error message returned by pveproxy on authentication failures due to wrong credentials, so a failed login does not leak whether the user exists.
  • Integrate MBEC and GMET support patches for the newer Linux kernel 7.0.
  • Fix several kernel issues:
    • Backport a fix for "vblank wait time out" events (issue 7585).
    • Backport a fix for a spurious, benign vmalloc warning on setups using ZFS.
    • Backport a fix for potential kernel crashes on setups using the mpt3sas kernel driver.
    • Backport fixes for an USB HID regression that could affect, among other things, APC UPS communication (issue 7550).
    • Backport a fix for potential kernel crashes on setups using ZFS when a process was killed because of a cgroup exceeding its memory limits.
    • Prevent a kernel panic by avoiding a NULL dereference in an apparmor unix socket check (issue 7083).
    • Avoid stalls in Proxmox Backup Server backup and sync connections by reverting problematic TCP changes in Linux kernel 6.17.
    • Integrate upstream fixes for the "Crackarmor" AppArmor vulnerabilities (PSA-2026-00010-1).
    • Integrate upstream fixes for the "copy.fail" local privilege escalation vulnerability via AF_ALG sockets (PSA-2026-00018-1).
    • Integrate upstream fixes for the "DirtyFrag" local privilege escalation vulnerability (PSA-2026-00019-1, PSA-2026-00019-2).
    • Integrate upstream fixes for the "Fragnesia" local privilege escalation vulnerability (PSA-2026-00020-1).
    • Integrate upstream fixes for the "ssh-keysign-pwn" vulnerability (PSA-2026-00021-1).
    • Integrate upstream fixes for the "pintheft" local privilege escalation vulnerability (PSA-2026-00022-1).

Known Issues & Breaking Changes

Transient upgrade issue with a disarmed HA stack

The HA stack can be disarmed since 5.1.3 to allow cluster-wide maintenance without risking node fencing. If it is disarmed while HA resources are still migrating to other nodes, the upgrade from a version before 5.2.4 can stall on the pve-ha-manager package triggers.

To avoid this, keep the HA stack armed during the upgrade or wait for all migrations to finish first; disabling automatic rebalancing beforehand stops new migrations from starting. If an upgrade does get stuck, simply re-arm the HA stack and it continues on its own.

Once 5.2.4 or newer is installed and running, no further upgrades are affected. Users of the enterprise repository were never affected, and any cluster that has not used the disarm feature is unaffected as well.

Systems hosted at OVH using proxmox-boot-tool boot into memtest86+

Proxmox VE 9.2 added memtest86+ as boot entry for systems using proxmox-boot-tool to manage the host's boot loader. Leading to systems at OVH, which were not installed from their template (but via IPMI) booting into memtest86+. This is due to the boot-process there using iPXE to optionally provide a rescue system, see the OVH documentation on their boot-process.

To keep in line with OVH recommendations you can set the efiBootloaderPath setting via their API to point to the boot-loader on the ESP. This can be best determined with efibootmgr -v and is likely \efi\proxmox\shimx64.efi or \efi\systemd\systemd-bootx64.efi depending on whether secure-boot is enabled or not respectively.

Systems not using proxmox-boot-tool (by default those that do not use ZFS or BTRFS on /) are not affected.

On Intel hosts, Windows 11/2022/2025 VMs with host CPU type and VBS enabled may intermittently freeze

On Intel hosts, Windows 11/2022/2025 guests using the host CPU type and Virtualization-based Security (VBS) enabled may occasionally freeze for a limited amount of time. While the VM is frozen, the VM summary will show 100% CPU usage. The exact conditions for a VM to be affected, among other information, can be found at Bugzilla #7825.

A fix is available in qemu-server 9.2.0, which introduces a new machine version 11.0+pve2. Existing VMs with certain machine versions may additionally require manual action to fix the issue:

  • Windows VMs using machine versions 11.0 or 11.0+pve1 are potentially affected by this issue. Such guests may have been created with the packages available in the Proxmox VE 9.2 ISO or qemu-server older than 9.2.0. Upgrading the qemu-server package and setting the machine version to 11.0+pve2 or newer should resolve the issue.
  • Windows VMs using machine version 11.0+pve2 or newer are not affected by this issue. Under qemu-server 9.2.0 or later, new Windows VMs are created with an unaffected machine version.
  • Windows VMs using machine versions older than 11.0 are not affected if using qemu-server 9.2.0 or newer.

Compatibility issues of installer ISO kernel and RAID controllers using the megaraid_sas driver in certain setups

Kernel 7.0.2-6, shipped with the ISO installer, has compatibility issues with RAID controllers using the megaraid_sas drivers and certain NVMe drives in JBOD mode. The issue has been fixed in kernel version 7.0.14-15. On affected setups, in order to avoid any installation problems or data integrity problems with the affected driver, it's recommended to use the Proxmox VE 9.1 ISO for installation and perform an upgrade to Proxmox VE 9.2 afterwards.

Proxmox VE 9.1

Released 19. November 2025: See Downloads

  • Based on Debian Trixie (13.2)
  • Latest 6.17.2-1 Kernel as new stable default
  • QEMU 10.1.2
  • LXC 6.0.5
  • ZFS 2.3.4
  • Ceph Squid 19.2.3

Highlights

  • Create LXC containers from OCI images.
    Open Container Initiative (OCI) images are a popular format for distributing templates for system or application containers.
    OCI images can now be uploaded manually or downloaded from image registries, and then be used as templates for LXC containers.
    This allows to create full system containers from suitable OCI images.
    Initial support for creating application containers from suitable OCI images is also available (technology preview).
  • Support for TPM state in qcow2 format.
    Some VM guest workloads require attaching a virtual Trusted Platform Module (TPM), for example newer Windows guests.
    The state of a virtual TPM can now be stored in the qcow2 format.
    This allows taking snapshots of VMs with a TPM state on file-level storages such as NFS or CIFS shares.
    Storages with "snapshots as volume chains" (technology preview) enabled now support taking offline snapshots of VMs with TPM state.
  • Fine-grained control of nested virtualization for VM guests.
    Some VM guest workloads need access to the host CPU's virtualization extensions for nested virtualization.
    Examples are nested hypervisors or Windows guests with Virtualization-based Security enabled.
    A new vCPU flag allows to enable nested virtualization on top of a vCPU type that corresponds to the host CPU vendor and generation.
    This can be an alternative to using the full host vCPU type.
  • More detailed status reporting for the Software-Defined Networking (SDN) stack in the GUI.
    Local bridges and VNets report the currently connected guests.
    EVPN zones additionally report the learned IPs and MAC addresses.
    Fabrics are now part of the resource tree and report routes, neighbors, and interfaces.
  • Kernel 6.17 as new stable default.
  • Seamless upgrade from Proxmox VE 8.4, see Upgrade from 8 to 9.

Changelog Overview

Before upgrading, please consider Known Issues & Breaking Changes.

Enhancements in the web interface (GUI)

  • Allow to initiate bulk actions directly from the Tag View.
    The Tag View provides a convenient overview over virtual guests, grouped according to their assigned tags.
    Right-clicking on a tag in the resource tree now allows to conveniently initiate bulk actions for virtual guests with that tag.
  • Right-clicking on a VM in the resource tree now allows to reset the VM (issue 4248).
  • Improvements to the new mobile web interface introduced in Proxmox VE 9.0:
    • Support login using an OpenID Connect (OIDC) realm.
    • The VM hardware and option panels now show pending changes.
    • Allow to edit VM options directly from the mobile web interface.
    • Improve detection of mobile Firefox (issue 6657).
    • Fix an issue where the consent banner was not rendered as Markdown.
  • Move the global search bar to the middle of the screen and adapt to the screen size for improved visibility.
  • Use icons that are more suitable for high-resolution display (issue 6599).
  • Increase the thresholds for warning about high memory usage for the cluster, node, and guest summary pages.
  • Fix an issue where no login dialog would be shown after a session has expired.
  • Fix an issue where resource pool members would not be displayed correctly after adding or removing a member (issue 6385).
  • Fix performance issues where the GUI would be slowed down in setups with many guests.
  • The datacenter, node, and guest summary pages now show two separate selectors for graph timeframe and aggregation type.
    Previously, both settings were combined into one dropdown box.
  • The journal viewer now keeps the horizontal scrolling position even when refreshing the journal (issue 6679).
  • Fix an issue where APT changelogs or package descriptions with non-ASCII characters would be displayed incorrectly.
  • Clarify that the graphs in the storage summary show usage in bytes.
  • Fix an issue where the first guest would not update its status in the resource tree for five minutes after creation.
  • Allow filtering for name, node or VMID when adding guests to a resource pool.
  • Fix an issue where certain setting dialogs, available only to highly-privileged users by default, were susceptible to XSS (PSA-2025-00013-1).
  • Fix an issue where the task description of some task types would be incomplete.
  • Fix a regression where the resource tree tags would not have the correct color (issue 6815).

Virtual machines (KVM/QEMU)

  • New QEMU version 10.1.2:
    See the upstream changelog for details.
  • Support for TPM state in qcow2 format.
    Some VM guest workloads require attaching a virtual Trusted Platform Module (TPM), for example newer Windows guests.
    The state of a virtual TPM can now be stored in a non-raw format such as qcow2, and is then provided to QEMU using the QEMU storage daemon.
    This allows taking snapshots of VMs with a TPM state on file-level storages such as directory, NFS and CIFS shares.
    Storages with "snapshots as volume chains" (technology preview) enabled now support taking offline snapshots of VMs with TPM state.
  • Fine-grained control of nested virtualization for VM guests.
    Some VM guest workloads need access to the host CPU's virtualization extensions for nested virtualization.
    Examples are nested hypervisors or Windows guests with Virtualization-based Security enabled.
    The host CPU type makes all CPU flags visible within the guest, which may not be desirable in all setups.
    As an alternative, a new vCPU flag nested-virt allows to specifically enable nested virtualization.
    The flag has to be enabled on a vCPU type corresponding to the host CPU vendor and generation, enabling it on a generic x86-64-v* vCPU type is not sufficient.
    nested-virt automatically resolves to the vendor-specific virtualization flag.
  • Add initial support for Intel Trust Domain Extensions (TDX).
    On supported platforms, such as specific recent Intel CPUs, TDX can isolate guest memory from the host.
    TDX also requires support in the guest. Windows guests currently do not support TDX.
    Initial support for enabling TDX attestation is also available.
    Some features like live migration are unsupported.
    With Intel TDX and AMD SEV, Proxmox VE now provides initial integration of all major vendor-specific confidential computing technologies.
  • Allow disabling Kernel Samepage Merging (KSM) for specific VMs (issue 5291).
    KSM can optimize memory usage in setups that run many similar VMs, but in some setups, it may be required to disable KSM.
    Instead of completely disabling KSM, it is now possible to disable KSM only for specific VMs.
  • Ensure newly created EFI disks contain new Microsoft UEFI CA 2023 keys to avoid guest boot failures with Secure Boot (issue 6985).
    Newer Windows ISOs will only be signed by this new CA and thus require the keys to be present.
    Existing EFI disks are currently not updated automatically to avoid issues with existing Windows installations, and need to be updated manually.
  • PCI passthrough: Improve compatibility with devices that are already bound to the correct VFIO driver.
    By default, PCI devices are bound to the vfio-pci driver and reset.
    Some devices, such as certain NICs or GPUs, may support targeted VFIO drivers that provide more features.
    The new driver option can be configured to keep the binding to the targeted VFIO drivers.
    This option is currently only available via API and CLI.
  • Pressing escape during early boot of an OVMF VM will now lead to a boot device menu rather than the "EFI Firmware Setup" menu.
    The reason is that choosing a different boot device is the most common use case.
    The "EFI Firmware Setup" can still be selected in the boot entry menu and reached from there.
  • The qm import CLI command now supports the import-from parameter.
  • Enable Joliet for cloud-init disks to avoid issues with the nocloud ISO format on Windows guests (issue 6989).
  • Remove notice marking AMD SEV-ES and SEV-SNP as highly experimental.
  • Avoid applying pending changes when resuming from hibernation (issue 6934).
  • Fix a short-lived regression where aarch64 VMs would not start with machine type 10.1 or higher (issue 7014).
  • Disable High Precision Event Timer (HPET) for recent Linux VMs and machine version 10.1 to avoid increased CPU usage.
  • Avoid failing live migration of certain VMs due to a different MTU settings on the source and target node.
  • Allow setting the guest-phys-bits CPU option, which may be required for PCI(e) passthrough on certain Intel CPUs (issue 6378).
  • Allow setting the aw-bits machine option to work around issues when using the Intel vIOMMU driver on newer machine versions (issue 6608).
  • Remote migration: Increase timeout for volume activation to avoid migration failures (issue 6828).
  • Ensure correct ordering of machine versions (issue 6648).
  • Fix an issue where a migration with conntrack state could fail due to unfortunate timing.
  • Fix an issue where FreeBSD guests could get stuck when canceling a SCSI request when using VirtIO SCSI (issue 6810).
  • Backport a fix for an issue where enabling the VNC clipboard for a Windows guest could cause the mouse pointer to get stuck.
  • Avoid blocking the QEMU guest agent if an fsfreeze command never returns.
    Such situations can arise if an in-guest shutdown is triggered after initiating the freeze, but before it is finished.
    This is implemented by querying the completion status multiple times instead of blocking the socket for up to one hour.
  • Integrate fixes for Spectre branch target injection ("VMScape", see PSA-2025-00016-1).
  • Fix an issue where the Disk IO graphs would show spikes if the QEMU monitor was unavailable when collecting statistics (issue 6207).
  • Fix an issue where a VM with an unavailable QEMU monitor would slow down statistics collection.
  • Fix a regression where VM template backups with QEMU machine version 10 and IDE/SATA with a read-only volume could fail (issue 6675).
  • Fix an issue where SCSI device passthrough would not work with QEMU machine version 10 (issue 6680).
  • Fix an issue where the CPU could not be edited if properties with dashes had been added manually to the VM CPU config.
  • Avoid a "timeout waiting on systemd" error after a live migration.
  • Avoid logging spurious QEMU monitor errors to the logs.

Containers (LXC)

  • Create LXC containers from OCI images.
    Open Container Initiative (OCI) images are a popular format for distributing templates for system or application containers.
    OCI images can now be uploaded manually or downloaded from image registries, and then be used as templates for LXC containers.
    This allows to create full system containers from suitable OCI images.
    Initial support for creating application containers from suitable OCI images is also available (technology preview).
    The entrypoint for containers can now be modified in the container options.
  • Customization of environment variables.
    The container options now allow to set environment variables that are passed to the container's entrypoint.
    This is especially useful for application containers that can be configured using environment variables.
  • Host-managed DHCP for containers without a network stack.
    For system containers, DHCP management is delegated to the network management stack shipped by the container.
    However, application containers may not ship a network management stack.
    Enabling host-managed DHCP allows such containers to acquire DHCP leases via the DHCP tooling on the host.
    Host-managed DHCP is automatically enabled for application containers created from OCI images.
  • New LXC version 6.0.5:
    See the upstream changelog for details.
  • Improve compatibility with guest distributions:
    • Add support for openSUSE Leap 16.0 (issue 6731).
    • Support Ubuntu 25.10 and 26.04 LTS releases, and warn instead of erroring out if the release is unknown.
    • Warn instead of erroring out if the Debian version in a container is higher than the known maximum.
    • Fix compatibility issues Debian 13 point releases.
    • Fix DHCP issues in Debian 13 containers by actively installing isc-dhcp-client and ifupdown2.
    • Disable systemd-networkd-wait-online.service in Debian 13 containers to prevent spurious error-messages and long delays during startup.
    • Improve compatibility with AlmaLinux 10 and CentOS 10 containers.
  • Lift restrictions on /proc and /sys if nesting is enabled to avoid issues in certain nested setups (issue 7006).
  • Regenerate snakeoil SSH certificates and keypairs shipped in Debian-based containers (PSA-2025-00017-1).
  • Show dynamically assigned IP addresses in the Network tab.
  • Document that systemd inside a container requires nesting to be enabled (issue 6897).
  • Enable Router Advertisements if the container is configured with DHCPv6 (issue 5538).

General improvements for virtual guests

  • Support for node-independent bulk actions.
    Bulk actions already allow to conveniently start, shut down, suspend, or migrate many virtual guests located on a specific node.
    In addition, it is now possible to initiate bulk actions on the datacenter level.
    The new datacenter-level bulk actions are also accessible by right-clicking on the datacenter in the resource tree.
  • Fix an issue where the API would not generate PNGs for RRD graphs.
  • Fix an overly strict permission check for NICs without a bridge.

HA Manager

  • Allow adding VMs and containers as HA resources after creation or restore.
    The HA state is automatically set to started if "Start after creation"/"Start after restore" is enabled.
  • When deleting a resource, also optionally purge it by updating all rules containing the resource (issue 6613).
  • Significantly reduce the time to compute the currently used resources for Cluster Resource Scheduling (CRS) with the static load scheduler (technology preview).

    • Add support for configuring resources for the static-load scheduler.
  • Fix an issue where disabled HA rules could not be re-enabled in the GUI.
  • Fix an issue where HA rules could not be edited in the GUI.
  • Support parsing version strings of nodes running legacy versions of Proxmox VE (< 8.0) in mixed clusters.
  • Fix a warning due to a wrong emptiness check for blocking resources (issue 6881).
  • Fix issues that would incorrectly mark rules as unsatisfiable, resulting from the false assumption to also consider ignored resources.
  • Apply negative affinity constraints before positive ones, in order not to limit the number of available nodes unneccessarily.
  • Remove the computed and obsolete group field from the service status data.

Improved management for Proxmox VE clusters

  • Improve reliability of opening shells for root@pam to other cluster nodes (issue 6789).
  • Improvements to the /cluster/metrics/export API endpoint for pull-style metric collection:
    • Use credentials from original request for proxying.

  • ACME Account view: Improve handling of providers that do not return an e-mail field, such as Let's Encrypt.

      Migrating RRD data when upgrading from Proxmox VE 8.4 is not necessary anymore.
    • Fix two glitches with the resolution calculation for the longer, aggregated timeframes.
    • Fix a timing issue when reading from RRD files before the metrics are written to it (issue 6615).

Backup/Restore

Storage

    This avoids issues in case a node had been shut down for a long time.

    • Clarify informational and warning messages.

    vncterm/spiceterm are spawned when a user with sufficient privileges initiates a VNC or SPICE session, for accessing a node or container console via the GUI.
